#### Selected angle scope

**Focus / example question:** What happens between an AI input and its final output?

**Coverage:** Follow one defined task through data preparation, model inference, output handling, and human checks.

**Evidence to seek:** original system papers, model or dataset documentation, reproducible evaluations and attributed contributor accounts appropriate to the selected question

**Limits:** Name the system version, task and evaluation conditions; distinguish a demonstration from general capability and verify deployment claims separately.

Before Phase 1, narrow this guidance to a specific case, source or work, and the relevant place, population, version or period. Record that choice in the episode's story question and research summary.

This is the selected angle's planning guidance at project creation. Use the episode story question to choose the concrete case; document its coverage and exclusions in Phase 1.

### Production summary — Original Transformer attention
Episode title: How Attention Works in the Original Transformer.
Question: How does the original Transformer combine token relationships, and where does the attention analogy stop?

Niche 001; IDEA-001-0001; TOP-001-001; SER-001-001 episode 1.
EN; general public; 600 seconds; 75 eight-second planned slots; eight narrative sections.

Use the source-bounded claim set C001–C013, an original vector example and a qualified discussion of the two retained research positions. No benchmark values, current-product specifications, trained-model measurements or universal cognition conclusion enter this package.

#### Decisions and invariants
Heavy visual priority; source documentation uses attributed paraphrases, with no interviews or invented quotations. Monetization is secondary. Essential precise graphics carry numerical meaning. External AI tools supply user-generated illustrative media; overlays supply exact text and mathematics. Provider and model are selected at generation time.

Retain A as cyan square, B as amber circle, C as magenta triangle. Keep A/B/C order left to right and vector coordinates horizontal. Display a synthetic-example label whenever toy numbers appear. C remains present but visibly excluded during the masking illustration.

Use Claim_Ledger.md for factual scope. Preserve C009/C010 attribution together and exclude benchmark numbers. Titles, thumbnails and narration follow the same limits.

## 3. Master Voiceover / Dialogue Script

### Narration scene 1

NARRATOR: Look at a diagram filled with bright connections. What, exactly, would you need to know before trusting its explanation?

### Narration scene 2

NARRATOR: Our question is deliberately narrow: how does attention combine information, and where should an explanation of that operation stop?

### Narration scene 3

NARRATOR: We will use the original Transformer paper, first submitted to arXiv in June two thousand seventeen.

### Narration scene 4

NARRATOR: We will also work through invented numbers. They are teaching materials, never measurements collected from a trained model.

### Narration scene 5

NARRATOR: Keep three questions separate: what is being calculated, what system contains that calculation, and what evidence supports an interpretation?

### Narration scene 6

NARRATOR: A useful explanation should let you inspect the steps. It should also tell you which conclusions those steps cannot establish.

### Narration scene 7

NARRATOR: Our small example will have three positions, which we will call A, B, and C. The labels carry no word meanings.

### Narration scene 8

NARRATOR: Instead of showing a mysterious machine, we will show supplied inputs, a calculation, and its resulting vector.

### Narration scene 9

NARRATOR: Later, we will change which positions are allowed into that calculation. Watch the normalization, rather than just the colored arrows.

### Narration scene 10

NARRATOR: Finally, we will ask why describing an operation and explaining a model's prediction require different kinds of evidence.

### Narration scene 11

NARRATOR: First, locate the operation. The original Transformer has an encoder and a decoder; this is our historical architecture.

### Narration scene 12

NARRATOR: Its attention appears in three settings: encoder self-attention, decoder self-attention, and attention connecting the decoder with the encoder.

### Narration scene 13

NARRATOR: Keep those settings distinct. A rule for one should not quietly become a rule for every part of the diagram.

### Narration scene 14

NARRATOR: For now, isolate one calculation. In scaled dot-product attention, query and key scores are scaled and normalized before values are combined.

### Narration scene 15

NARRATOR: Think of query, key, and value as labels for mathematical roles. Our diagram should preserve those labels throughout the demonstration.

### Narration scene 16

NARRATOR: A score is not the final combined result. Follow each stage rather than treating every highlighted connection as the same quantity.

### Narration scene 17

NARRATOR: The normalization step belongs between scoring and combining. We will make its arithmetic visible rather than hiding it behind an animation.

### Narration scene 18

NARRATOR: Our supplied vectors are already prepared for this operation. We are not demonstrating how a trained model learned them.

### Narration scene 19

NARRATOR: That boundary matters: checking an example's arithmetic cannot tell us which words a real model would connect in a real task.

### Narration scene 20

NARRATOR: We can inspect each quantity slowly while keeping the limit clear: this example does not observe an actual model.

### Narration scene 21

NARRATOR: Here are invented inputs. The label reminds us that these numbers were chosen for teaching, rather than measured from a model.

### Narration scene 22

NARRATOR: The query is square root of two times natural log of two, followed by zero. The exact expression stays on screen.

### Narration scene 23

NARRATOR: The keys for A, B, and C are one, zero; zero, one; and zero, zero, respectively.

### Narration scene 24

NARRATOR: For each key, take its dot product with the query, then divide by the square root of two.

### Narration scene 25

NARRATOR: The resulting scores are natural log of two, zero, and zero. These are intermediate scores, not the final weights.

### Narration scene 26

NARRATOR: Now exponentiate those scores. For our chosen numbers, the three contributions are two, one, and one.

### Narration scene 27

NARRATOR: Their total is four. Divide each contribution by that total so we can inspect the normalized weights.

### Narration scene 28

NARRATOR: A receives one half. B receives one quarter. C receives one quarter. In this invented example, those weights sum to one.

### Narration scene 29

NARRATOR: Pause on the fractions. The colors should help you follow the calculation, while the labels preserve its exact values.

### Narration scene 30

NARRATOR: Nothing here was discovered by asking a model a question. We selected the inputs so that you could check every step.

### Narration scene 31

NARRATOR: Next come the values. A has the vector two, zero. B has zero, four. C has zero, zero.

### Narration scene 32

NARRATOR: Multiply A's value by its weight of one half. Its contribution to the combined vector is one, zero.

### Narration scene 33

NARRATOR: Multiply B's value by one quarter. Its contribution is zero, one. C's weighted contribution remains zero, zero.

### Narration scene 34

NARRATOR: Adding these contributions gives the combined vector one, one. The result follows directly from the supplied values and weights.

### Narration scene 35

NARRATOR: This distinction is worth keeping: a weight describes a coefficient in the combination; the combined vector is a different object.

### Narration scene 36

NARRATOR: The output is two numbers. Our illustration has neither a prediction head nor a trained network interpreting a sentence.

### Narration scene 37

NARRATOR: Try tracing the result backward on the diagram. Which value contributed to the first coordinate, and which to the second?

### Narration scene 38

NARRATOR: In these invented numbers, A supplies the first coordinate and B supplies the second. The labels make the calculation inspectable.

### Narration scene 39

NARRATOR: You can verify that arithmetic without making a claim about language understanding, conscious attention, or the cause of a model's prediction.

### Narration scene 40

NARRATOR: Now keep the same supplied vectors. We will change the allowed positions and calculate the weights again.

### Narration scene 41

NARRATOR: The original decoder's self-attention masks future positions before softmax. This does not describe every attention setting in the architecture.

### Narration scene 42

NARRATOR: For our separate masking illustration, place the query at B. Allow A and B, and exclude C from the combination.

### Narration scene 43

NARRATOR: The allowed exponential contributions are now two and one. The excluded position contributes zero to their normalization total.

### Narration scene 44

NARRATOR: The total is three. The new weights are two thirds, one third, and zero, in the order A, B, C.

### Narration scene 45

NARRATOR: Notice the important change: we normalized again over allowed positions. We did not simply erase the old quarter assigned to C.

### Narration scene 46

NARRATOR: If we erased that old weight without normalizing again, the remaining weights would total three quarters rather than one.

### Narration scene 47

NARRATOR: Using the new weights, A contributes four thirds to the first coordinate. B contributes four thirds to the second.

### Narration scene 48

NARRATOR: The masked example therefore produces four thirds, four thirds. Both its weights and its result are shown as exact fractions.

### Narration scene 49

NARRATOR: These are two versions of an invented calculation, not an experiment showing that masking improves a trained model's performance.

### Narration scene 50

NARRATOR: What you can inspect is the operation: the allowed positions changed, the normalization changed, and the weighted sum changed.

### Narration scene 51

NARRATOR: Before leaving the diagram, put the operation back in context. This small calculation does not represent the whole original architecture.

### Narration scene 52

NARRATOR: Multi-head attention joins projected head outputs and projects their concatenation. Our single-query illustration leaves that larger structure out.

### Narration scene 53

NARRATOR: Avoid giving a head a permanent human-like job based on a decorative illustration. This episode has not measured such a job.

### Narration scene 54

NARRATOR: The original design also adds positional encodings to input embeddings. Keep this architectural step separate from our supplied toy inputs.

### Narration scene 55

NARRATOR: Feed-forward sublayers, residual connections, and normalization also belong to the architecture. An attention-only picture needs that visible boundary.

### Narration scene 56

NARRATOR: A simplified drawing is useful when you can identify both the operation it shows and the parts it leaves out.

### Narration scene 57

NARRATOR: Remember the three attention settings. Their different places in the architecture help you keep the described operations distinct.

### Narration scene 58

NARRATOR: Return to the full diagram with the same labels. Keep the historical system identified throughout the architecture explanation.

### Narration scene 59

NARRATOR: The explanation remains about a selected design and a selected operation. It is not a specification for every product using AI.

### Narration scene 60

NARRATOR: That prepares the final question: even with the mechanism visible, what would justify treating its attention weights as an explanation?

### Narration scene 61

NARRATOR: Jain and Wallace investigated that question in studied attention-based NLP models. Their model scope must travel with their reported result.

### Narration scene 62

NARRATOR: They found substantially different attention distributions that could accompany similar predictions. Their finding concerns the systems studied in their experiments.

### Narration scene 63

NARRATOR: Our invented arithmetic does not reproduce that experiment. It cannot independently confirm or refute their conclusions about model explanations.

### Narration scene 64

NARRATOR: Wiegreffe and Pinter argued that assessing attention as explanation depends on a definition of explanation and model-aware testing.

### Narration scene 65

NARRATOR: That response asks us to define what would count as an explanation before deciding how to test it.

### Narration scene 66

NARRATOR: Read the two positions together. Ask which model, which prediction, and which criterion of explanation each discussion concerns.

### Narration scene 67

NARRATOR: Neither title should become a universal slogan about every attention head. Keep the claim as narrow as the evidence inspected here.

### Narration scene 68

NARRATOR: A bright heatmap can invite a story. Before accepting that story, ask what test connects the diagram to the interpretation.

### Narration scene 69

NARRATOR: For this episode, the result is a careful question rather than a claim that a mathematical picture reveals human-like thought.

### Narration scene 70

NARRATOR: We can describe an operation, check an example, and still require further evidence before making a claim about a model's reasoning.

### Narration scene 71

NARRATOR: Ask three questions of the next diagram: what system is shown, what quantity appears, and what evidence supports its meaning?

### Narration scene 72

NARRATOR: Keep the synthetic label on invented examples. Keep the source and its limits with any result reported by a study.

### Narration scene 73

NARRATOR: Our arithmetic showed supplied weights combining supplied values. It did not measure a trained model's understanding or settle the explanation debate.

### Narration scene 74

NARRATOR: The source links let you inspect the historical design and both research positions. Follow them when you want the precise context.

### Narration scene 75

NARRATOR: Understanding the steps is useful. Knowing exactly where those steps stop supporting a conclusion is part of understanding them well.

## 4. Company / Person Reference Sheet

### Character and world continuity
#### Period and setting
WORLD-01 is a timeless abstract editorial workspace; BOARD-01 is its arithmetic board. Source identity is historical, while the setting is an illustrative stage. Source cards distinguish 2017 work from later research; no period-reenactment footage is planned.

#### Entities
| ID | Appearance / role | Continuity constraint |
|---|---|---|
| A | Cyan square, left | Always position A; coordinate order remains horizontal |
| B | Amber circle, center | Query-associated position for the separate masked example |
| C | Magenta triangle, right | Stays present when excluded; hatch and label instead of deletion |
| NARRATOR-01 | Off-screen licensed/user voice | No real researcher imitation; one coherent delivery |
| SOURCE-01 | Original paper's text attribution | Plain typography, identified version, no counterfeit screenshot |
| SOURCE-02 / SOURCE-03 | Attributed 2019 positions | Equivalent visual emphasis; retain differing scope |

Markers are abstract positions, never characters with motives. No human likeness or biographical reenactment is required. The synthetic label remains visible for invented numbers; source cards never imply original footage.

#### Persistence
Keep marker shape, order, palette, camera orientation and vector-axis order across cuts and generation batches. Save actual reference files and provenance later in ASSET_REGISTER.md. If the generator changes identity or introduces false text, replace that output or remove it from the precise diagram layer.


### Viewer glossary and delivery plan
Explain terms on first spoken use, pair symbols with labels and retain a readable transcript. Definitions are limited to the cited operation/design; source references identify their scope.

| Term | Episode wording / limit | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Query / key / value | Mathematical roles in the selected attention operation; names are not human intentions | C003, S1 section 3.2 |
| Score | Intermediate scaled query–key quantity, before normalization | C003; local derivation |
| Weight | Normalized coefficient used to combine supplied values | C003/C011 |
| Vector | Ordered coordinates shown in the original toy calculation | Local synthetic example; no semantic meaning assigned |
| Softmax | Operation illustrated through exponentiation and normalization in the toy data | C003/C011; S1 equation 1 |
| Mask | Exclusion applied before normalization in the separate decoder illustration | C006/C013 |
| Self-attention / encoder–decoder attention | Distinct applications within the selected historical architecture | C005; S1 section 3.2.3 |
| Multi-head | Projected head outputs joined and projected as specified in the cited design | C004 |
| Positional encoding | Added positional information in the selected input representation | C007 |
| Explanation | A question requiring an explicit criterion and model/test context | C009/C010; attributed debate |

Delivery: retain these terms in transcript/captions, expand abbreviations on first use and verify exact numerical wording. Do not add glossary definitions for cognition or current products without new research.

### Visual style — Futuristic Tech Editorial
#### Design system
Use a restrained dark editorial workspace, charcoal #111827 background, off-white #F8FAFC text and generous empty space. A is cyan #22D3EE with a square; B is amber #FBBF24 with a circle; C is magenta #E879F9 with a triangle. Shapes, labels and placement redundantly identify positions; color alone never carries a distinction.

Plan a 1920×1080, 16:9 composition. Keep important elements inside a 96-pixel margin. Main labels target 48 pixels or larger; citations target 30 pixels or larger, with an enlarged source card when needed. These are creative layout targets, subject to actual readability review.

#### Layers
AI-generated material supplies abstract atmospheric surfaces or motion. Exact vector cards, diagram arrows, fractions and source labels are editorial layers. Use a clean, largely static board for arithmetic. Keep a persistent “Synthetic example” ribbon during cues 7–9 and 18–50.

Distinguish query card, keys, weights, values and combined vector through both position and labels. The score-to-weight transition changes the header explicitly. Maintain A/B/C order. An excluded C is hatched and marked “Excluded,” retaining its place.

Source cards use plain typographic attribution without logos, author portraits, fabricated paper screenshots or signatures. Avoid brain imagery, real-looking laboratories, science-fiction interfaces and invented empirical heatmaps.

#### Continuity
Use WORLD-01 and BOARD-01 throughout. Only the active operation moves; hold other values stable. End the masked sequence with both results separately labeled. All artwork remains planned.


### Numerical and diagram specifications
#### Authoritative inputs
[Research_Calculations.json](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Research_Calculations.json) and [its derivation](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Facts_Documented.md) define all toy data.

| Stage | Exact display |
|---|---|
| Query | q = (√2 × ln 2, 0); dₖ = 2 |
| Keys A/B/C | (1,0); (0,1); (0,0) |
| Values A/B/C | (2,0); (0,4); (0,0) |
| Scaled scores | (ln 2, 0, 0) |
| Exponential contributions | (2,1,1) |
| Unmasked normalization total | 4 |
| Unmasked weights | (1/2,1/4,1/4) |
| Combined output | (1,1) |
| Allowed contributions after excluding C | (2,1,0); total 3 |
| Masked weights and output | (2/3,1/3,0); (4/3,4/3) |

Use fractions, not rounded decimals, for thirds. If bars accompany weights, use a fixed 0-to-1 scale, baseline zero and constant widths. Label weights as coefficients in the synthetic operation, never measured word importance. No percentages, benchmark charts or reported performance panels are planned.

#### Animation acceptance
Reveal scores, exponentials, total, normalized weights and combined values in that order. C's exclusion changes normalization before weights update. Do not drop its original quarter and preserve the other old weights. Animate each coordinate separately for the weighted sum.

A source/year reference and synthetic label stay readable. Visual generation supplies no exact math. Reconcile rendered text and all diagram quantities against the calculation before accepting any real asset.


### Camera and animation plan
#### Framing
Use locked overhead BOARD-01 framing for numerical steps. Place cards on a stable left-to-right grid with one reserved lower band for captions and one source-label area. Architecture overviews may use slow controlled reframing; use a wide reset before each new section.

#### Transition and continuity
Prefer cuts, short dissolves or single-element reveals. End each shot with a readable hold. During arithmetic, the camera stays fixed while only the active coefficient, vector or result changes. Keep prior contributions visible when a sum is being built.

Preserve A/B/C positions and exact order. For masking, hatch C before normalization updates and retain distinct unmasked/masked result labels. No flashes, rapid zooms, spinning equations or unlabeled scale changes.

#### Generation instructions
Prompts specify a start state, one movement and end state for each scene. Generated backdrops remain text-free; precise overlays are handled separately. Reference WORLD-01/BOARD-01 and the asset IDs. Choose provider-specific syntax after inspecting that tool's current capabilities. No universal provider-duration or seed behavior is assumed.

Review actual generated continuity and motion before calling any asset reviewed. Phase 2 completes the plan, not the footage.


### Planned asset register
Status for every asset: planned. No media file, rights clearance or actual scene review is asserted. Scene numbers below are reserved slots; Phase 3 must reconcile actual scene use and manifest mappings.

| Asset ID | Planned asset | Scene slots | Target reference / provenance | Rights / status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G01 | Original abstract workspace | 1–75 | graphics/G01_workspace.png; original design, user external generation | Pending actual provider/reference review; planned |
| G02 | Historical architecture schematic | 11–20, 51–60 | graphics/G02_architecture.svg; original diagram from cited mechanism | Original artwork planned; source attribution retained |
| G03 | Exact synthetic inputs and weights | 7–9, 18–50 | graphics/G03_toy.svg; local calculation inputs | Authored data; overlay file not created |
| G04 | Exact arithmetic and result overlays | 21–50 | graphics/G04_arithmetic.svg; local calculation and derivation | Authored arithmetic; file planned |
| G05 | Source/interpretation cards | 3, 10, 60–70, 72, 74 | graphics/G05_sources.svg; citations and attributed paraphrases | Original typography planned; no quoted figure |
| G06 | Chapter/question cards | 1–75 as needed | graphics/G06_cards.svg; original editorial wording | Planned |
| V01 | Continuity reference board | 1–75 | references/V01_board.png; palette/shapes/layout | Planned, no reference image exists yet |
| A01 | Full narration | 1–75 | audio/A01_narration.wav; approved user/licensed voice | Rights and measured timing Pending; planned |
| A02 | Optional ambient bed | 11, 51, 61, 71 | audio/A02_bed.wav; actual creator/provider Pending | May be omitted; no license assumed; planned |
| C01 | EN captions/transcript | 1–75 | captions/C01_en.srt; actual audio aligned later | Planned, no timed captions created |
| T01 | Thumbnail | Release asset; no scene requirement | distribution/T01_thumbnail.png; original composition | Planned |

#### Scene coverage and lifecycle
G01, V01 and A01 map to every planned slot, so the manifest has complete planned coverage without claiming generated files. Optional A02 is not required for coverage. C01 is a deliverable planned after narration; its file extension is a target, not proof of export.

When generation happens, record actual tool/model/version, settings, prompt/reference inputs, date, creator, source/rights evidence and actual file location. Retain attempts and review findings. Change an asset to created only when its in-episode file exists; reviewed additionally needs review evidence. Use the existing manifest schema. No status may be inferred from this production plan alone.

#### Phase 3 reconciliation — P3-1

All 75 text scene records now exist. The ranges above match their asset references and the manifest. G05 also supports scene 72's source cards. Optional A02 is restricted to scenes 11, 51, 61 and 71; arithmetic scenes specify silence. No SFX asset is currently used. Files, actual licensing and media review remain Pending external generation.

#### Excluded research items

No unresolved material claim is retained in the script set. Benchmark-score discrepancies, detailed S3 experiments, claims about current products and universal cognition assertions are excluded in Research_Summary.md. Adding any of them requires new evidence and a reviewed ledger revision.


### Documented facts and original calculation

Research date: 2026-10-18. Use [Claim_Ledger.md](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Claim_Ledger.md) for exact factual wording, sources and confidence. This file organizes that evidence without introducing additional historical or empirical claims.

#### Evidence groups

| Research section | Claim IDs | Editorial use |
|---|---|---|
| Historical source identity | C001 | Orient to the dated source |
| Mechanism and architecture | C002–C008 | Explain the selected operation and its place in the system |
| Explanation limits | C009–C010 | Present attributed positions together |
| Original arithmetic | C011–C013 | Demonstrate transparent numbers with explicit limits |

#### T1: invented one-query example

This is original educational data. A, B and C label abstract positions, not words, semantic classes or actual model tokens. The query is associated with position B for the masking example. We supply projected vectors directly; we do not claim they were learned. No trained model was run.

Define one query `q = (sqrt(2) * ln(2), 0)` and key dimension `d_k = 2`.

| Position | Key | Value |
|---|---|---|
| A | (1, 0) | (2, 0) |
| B | (0, 1) | (0, 4) |
| C | (0, 0) | (0, 0) |

For this example, apply `score_i = dot(q, key_i) / sqrt(2)`. The scores are `(ln(2), 0, 0)`. Their exponentials are `(2, 1, 1)` and their sum is 4. Normalization therefore gives `(1/2, 1/4, 1/4)`.

The weighted sum is `(1/2)*(2,0) + (1/4)*(0,4) + (1/4)*(0,0) = (1,1)`.

For a separate causal-mask illustration at B, exclude C before normalization. Treat its exponential contribution as zero; the allowed contributions sum to 3. The weights become `(2/3, 1/3, 0)` and the weighted sum becomes `(4/3, 4/3)`.

Simply erasing C's weight after the original normalization would leave a total of 3/4; that is not the masked calculation above. Do not animate this shortcut as equivalent.

#### Checks and limits

Python standard-library `math` evaluated the dot products, scale, exponentials, normalization and sums. Results were compared with the fractions above at absolute tolerance 1e-12, with four component-wise comparisons passing. Floating-point thirds are stored as approximations in [Research_Calculations.json](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Research_Calculations.json); use exact fractions in editorial labels where legible.

This calculation establishes only the arithmetic for supplied vectors. It does not establish that a model associates a word with a meaning, that a weight causes a full-network prediction, or that masking improves any task result. It omits training, multiple heads, other layers, dropout and a prediction head. These omissions must remain explicit in downstream instructions.

#### Downstream acceptance criteria

- Preserve the label “synthetic example” wherever its numbers appear.
- Keep weights visually distinct from value vectors and the final combined vector.
- Keep the unmasked and masked examples distinct; do not imply they are an empirical before/after intervention.
- Use C009–C010 for the attributed methodological discussion, rather than using this toy example to settle it.
- Check all numerical overlays against the JSON and exact expressions before accepting a scene draft.

### Research summary — Original Transformer attention

Research date: 2026-10-18. Researcher: Codex. Stage: Phase 1 internal research review; no publication approval asserted.

#### Identity and question

- Idea: IDEA-001-0001, using the creation-time snapshot in CONFIG.
- Topic: TOP-001-001, Attention and sequence representation.
- Series: SER-001-001, Reading an AI claim, proposed episode 1.
- Angle: how_it_works, resolved through the registered angle preset.
- Question: How does the original Transformer combine token relationships, and where does the attention analogy stop?
- Audience: general adult viewers; define symbols and specialist terms before using them.
- Runtime: 600 seconds in CONFIG; future scene plan is 75 slots of 8 seconds. No scenes are authored or locked in this phase.

#### Research conclusion

Use the original architecture as a bounded mechanism example, with an independently checked synthetic calculation. Treat explainability as a separate research question. The two 2019 primary studies support a qualified discussion rather than a universal verdict. The script must attribute each study and its scope. See C001–C013 in [Claim_Ledger.md](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Claim_Ledger.md); those entries are the factual wording authority.

#### Why this is useful

The editorial goal is to help a viewer distinguish an operation, an illustration and evidence about a prediction. Success means the viewer can follow a small calculation and identify what it leaves out. This is an educational objective, not a measured audience outcome or claim about current products.

The challenge for production is explaining abstraction without assigning intentions to the model. Use language such as score, weight, combine and representation. If an analogy is introduced, identify which operation it illustrates and end the analogy before making a claim about understanding, consciousness or causation.

#### Coverage

1. Orient the viewer to the selected source and the limited object being explained.
2. Explain one operation using the original synthetic example in [Facts_Documented.md](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Facts_Documented.md).
3. Connect that example to the architecture through C002–C008 without presenting it as the entire network.
4. Introduce the explanation debate through C009–C010 and the methodological limits below.
5. Close with questions a viewer should ask before treating an illustrative diagram as evidence.

These are narrative research sections, not scene assignments. Phase 2 chooses pacing and visual treatment; Phase 3 must replace the ledger's section mappings with real scene IDs.

#### Exclusions

- No current chatbot architecture, vendor ranking or claim that all AI systems share this design.
- No model training, deployment demonstration, token-importance experiment or replication of published experiments.
- No reading human intentions into attention weights or claiming a universal result about consciousness.
- No benchmark score, comparative speed, training-cost figure or market figure.
- No generated image presented as an archival figure, instrument measurement or actual attention map.

#### Source and method review

S1: original paper, identified version and exact sections. S2: primary experiment report; abstract, assumptions and relevant experiment passages inspected. S3: primary methodological response; publisher abstract and bibliographic record inspected, with PDF access unreliable. S3 wording is restricted to the explicit abstract-level position; no detailed result is claimed. See [Source_List_with_Links.md](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Source_List_with_Links.md).

T1: original toy arithmetic, checked using Python math against exact fractions. Its numerical data are reproducible inputs chosen for this explanation, not observations. Four component-wise comparisons passed at tolerance 1e-12; [Research_Calculations.json](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Research_Calculations.json) records inputs and outputs.

The sources are primary evidence for their own methods or reported positions. They are not independent confirmation of each other's experiments. Source checking does not reproduce training or settle the interpretability debate.

#### Conflict handling

The inspected S1 HTML has inconsistent English–French benchmark values between its abstract/table and running text in section 6.1. Benchmark values are outside this episode and excluded entirely. Do not import them into narration or metadata. If that scope changes, reconcile an authoritative version before drafting the new claim.

For explainability, present C009 and C010 together. The source scope and comparison criteria matter; neither paper is presented as a direct test of every head in the original Transformer, or of current large language models. A defensible educational conclusion is to demand an appropriate test before treating a diagram as a complete explanation.

#### Safety, bias and rights review

No personal allegation, medical advice, financial advice, sponsorship or personal data is planned. Authors are attributed for their published work, without speculating about motives. The interpretability disagreement is disclosed in Q10, so CONFIG retains human review pending before publication. Internal research acceptance cannot supply that approval.

Prepare original explanatory artwork later. No third-party image, quotation, voice, music or footage reuse has been cleared by this phase. Refer to source publications for attribution; a source URL is not a media license. External AI generation will be performed by the user after the text/scene/prompt handoff.

#### Internal exit review

All 13 retained ledger entries were checked within their stated evidence scope. No retained claim relies on an unchecked passage. The source discrepancy is excluded; detailed S3 results are excluded. Financial verification is not applicable with reasons recorded. Timeline and numerical checks are documented. Phase 2 production decisions remain draft; no completion certificate or audiovisual output exists.

### Source credibility for production
Use the primary-source hierarchy already established in the research bible. The original paper supports its described architecture. Jain and Wallace support the reported finding in their studied model/task scope. Wiegreffe and Pinter support the explicit position in the inspected publisher abstract.

A research paper is primary evidence for what its authors report; it is not independent replication. The inspected source depth differs and must remain visible. Detailed S3 experiment claims require further reading before inclusion.

The synthetic example is original arithmetic. Its calculation checks cannot validate learned representations, word meanings, empirical importance or task performance. Show source cards and illustration labels separately.

No generated answer, search snippet, decorative image, CAT preset or topic map becomes a factual citation. If an upstream source changes or an error is reported, reopen the relevant ledger entry and affected scenes. Source refresh is required before final compilation.


## 7. YouTube Metadata

### YouTube editorial strategy
Purpose: a standalone ten-minute educational episode in the proposed “Reading an AI claim” series. Use the specific Transformer mechanism and the labeled synthetic example as the viewer promise. Follow YOUTUBE_METADATA.md for proposed copy.

Avoid a universal “AI thinks” headline or a claim that research has settled every interpretation question. Introduce the question promptly, show the source and example boundaries, and place chapter changes at planned scene boundaries.

No reach, retention, ranking or monetization performance is predicted. The title, thumbnail and description should describe the actual episode. Tags are a small descriptive aid; [YouTube's tag guidance](https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/146402?hl=en), checked 2026-10-18, emphasizes the main metadata over tags.

Short-form adaptations remain optional and separate. Use a self-contained explanation of the synthetic example with its label and source context; do not clip away an interpretation caveat. No Shorts/Reels file or upload is part of Phase 2.


### YouTube metadata — proposed release copy
Revision P2-1, 2026-10-18. Copy is prepared; uploading and publication remain unperformed. Planned chapter times require verification against the final edit.

#### Primary title
How Attention Works in the Original Transformer

#### Alternative titles
- Transformer Attention Explained with Invented Numbers
- What an Attention Diagram Can—and Cannot—Show
Use one title at release. No A/B experiment has run and no performance uplift is claimed.

#### Description
How does attention combine information in the original Transformer? Follow a clearly labeled synthetic example, then examine the limits of treating an attention diagram as an explanation.

The numbers are invented for teaching; they are not measurements from a trained model. This episode discusses a historical architecture and two attributed research positions within their stated scope.

Sources:
Attention Is All You Need — https://arxiv.org/html/1706.03762v7
Attention is not Explanation — https://aclanthology.org/N19-1357/
Attention is not not Explanation — https://aclanthology.org/D19-1002/

The original example's arithmetic is documented in the research package. No current product capabilities or benchmark rankings are asserted.

Production-method line for use after it matches actual execution: “Illustrative media was created using AI generation tools; exact mathematical overlays and source references were reviewed separately.” Confirm actual workflow before inserting that line into a published description.

#### Chapters
00:00 The question and the limits
01:20 Where the operation belongs

### Monetization plan
Secondary priority; CONFIG records AdSense revenue as the intended model. This plan does not establish channel eligibility, advertiser suitability or revenue.

No sponsor, affiliate promotion, paid endorsement, sales offer or invented researcher endorsement is included. Avoid product ranking and claims that a specific tool reproduces the research.

Publication and monetization decisions remain with the user after the text package, media review and required human review. No account was inspected and no settings were changed.


### Originality and overlap review
#### Scope
Internal review of the selected idea, the five-niche depth plan and directly neighboring ideas, with source scope preserved. No global semantic search or worldwide originality clearance was performed.

#### Overlap
IDEA-001-0002 concerns dataset provenance; IDEA-001-0003 concerns model reporting/evaluation. IDEA-531-0001 concerns workflow/agent architecture. The present episode focuses on one historical attention operation and a bounded interpretation question. Shared AI terminology alone does not make these the same episode.

Use a link to future related work only when that episode exists and has been reviewed. Do not repeat the full arithmetic demonstration in every series installment.

#### Original contribution and limits
The inputs, arithmetic presentation, editorial sequence and teaching questions are authored for this episode. Paper titles and sources are attributed. No paper figure, researcher likeness or actual attention heatmap is reused. Original explanatory structure is not a claim that the underlying mechanism is newly discovered.

Actual generated assets still require provenance and similarity/rights review. This text review cannot clear images or voices that have not been produced.


## 8. Legal Review Summary

### Rights and review plan
The episode uses attributed technical paraphrases and original arithmetic. No allegations, personal data, interviews, real-person reenactments or voice imitations are planned.

#### Rights record
Original diagrams are the visual plan. Preserve publication citations. Any later figure, quotation, photograph, music, voice or footage reuse needs a separate rights/attribution entry in ASSET_REGISTER.md. The ability to access a publication is not a blanket asset permission. External provider terms and reference-input rights must be checked when the user chooses the tool.

#### Human publication review
CONFIG requires human review because Q10 includes a methodological disagreement. Status remains Pending. An internal completed document or automated gate does not supply approval. A named, dated reviewer must examine the final wording and applicable release checks before publication status.

#### Disclosure
The description identifies synthetic numerical examples and proposes a production-method note. Actual YouTube AI-use disclosure is chosen against the generated media, using current official guidance; see YOUTUBE_METADATA.md. No legal clearance, licenses or platform eligibility are asserted by this plan.


### Ethical storytelling decisions
Represent the original design as a specific historical architecture. Represent each paper's conclusions at the inspected scope. Attribution follows the research position throughout the narration and metadata.

Synthetic examples have persistent labels; authorial teaching choices are not observations. Never fabricate a learned attention heatmap, laboratory evidence, researcher quotation, interview, endorsement or archival scene.

The explanation debate is presented through C009 and C010 together. Use paired text cards with equivalent visual emphasis, while preserving their different evidence/inspection scope. This is a contextual comparison, not a staged adversarial encounter.

Educational questions in the narration invite inspection. They do not measure comprehension or promise that the audience now understands all Transformer systems. Content revisions that change the conclusion reopen the research gate.

After generation, inspect whether visual style implies claims absent from narration. Replace misleading footage rather than relying on a fine-print disclaimer. Record revisions and retain original evidence.


### Bias review — planned content
Internal editorial review: 2026-10-18.

| Question | Decision/evidence |
|---|---|
| Is a current product substituted for the selected historical design? | No; architecture framing remains bounded |
| Is one researcher or team portrayed as infallible? | No; publishable claims retain source scope |
| Are both retained interpretability positions represented? | Yes; paired cues 61–67 |
| Is a study generalized to every attention head? | Explicitly excluded in cues and guides |
| Are invented numbers presented as measurements? | No; persistent synthetic label and local provenance |
| Does the title imply a settled universal cognition claim? | No; mechanism-specific title chosen |
| Are source-inspection limitations disclosed? | Yes; S3 abstract-only limit retained |
| Is the content disguised promotion? | No sponsorship or affiliate content is planned |
| Are actual generated depictions reviewed? | Pending generation; inspect implication as well as text |

The checklist accepts the planned wording and treatment internally. It does not represent external peer review, measured neutrality or review of media that has not been generated.

#### Change protocol

##### Revision P2-2 / scene revision P3-1 — 2026-10-18

Reviewer: Codex, internal text/editorial role. Five cues (21, 56, 57, 62 and 65) were smoothed from production-oriented phrasing into viewer-facing narration. Current script length is 1,448 whitespace-delimited words. The master script, manifest narration and corresponding scenes were revised together. Claims, scientific scope, chapter boundaries, eight-second durations and Q3/Q15/Q16/Q42 are unchanged.

Seventy-five scene records and distinct prompt actions were instantiated in three batches. C012 was added as a visual reference in ten masking-related scenes where the unmasked output remains visible; researched claim wording and sources are unchanged. Beat claim unions and ledger scene mappings were reconciled. G05 adds scene 72 and optional A02 is restricted to non-arithmetic chapter transitions. Every media asset remains planned. Phase 0–3 validation passed before final consistency checks; final results are in STATUS.md and AUDIT/AI_ATTENTION_PHASE3_CHECKS.json.
A later revision records date, author/reviewer role, reason, affected cues/scenes/claims/assets, changed configuration locks if any, and verification result. Update both script representations together. Research changes reopen Phase 1 and regenerate downstream work. After Phase 4, recompile when inputs change.

Phase 2 status means the plan was internally accepted. Phase 3, actual generation, media review, human approval and publication have independent states. Final validation results are recorded in STATUS.md and the audit handoff.

## 10. Final Pre-Generation Checklist

### Production accuracy review
Internal review date: 2026-10-18; scope is text and planned treatment.

| Check | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Identity/question matches CONFIG snapshot | Checked | 00_Project_Consistency_Rules.md and CONFIG |
| Every factual narration cue has research references | Checked | 11_Dialogue_Script.md / PRODUCTION_MANIFEST.json (narration_plan); editorial questions carry no new factual assertion |
| Original design distinguished from present products | Checked | Historical framing in cues 3, 11 and 58–59 |
| Original numerical inputs and exact fractions preserved | Checked | Cues 21–50 and Research_Calculations.json |
| Mask applies before normalization | Checked | Cues 43–46; C013 |
| Explanation positions attributed and scoped | Checked | Cues 61–67; C009/C010 |
| Benchmark discrepancy excluded | Checked | No score/cost is present |
| Source and illustration distinctions retained | Checked | Persistent synthetic labels and scope cards |
| Timeline language uses supported precision | Checked | June 2017; no invented day in narration |
| Actual media, audio alignment and captions | Pending generation | DELIVERY_CHECKLIST.md |
| Human publication review | Pending | CONFIG review gate |

Phase 3 must inspect each final scene and map all factual wording to claim IDs, including on-screen text and visual implications. Actual scene citation coverage, measured audio and real exports cannot be accepted from a planning document. Any new material assertion requires a ledger revision before use.


### Financial data applicability
**Not applicable:** this technical educational episode contains no financial figures, financial advice, product price, training-cost comparison or market claim.

Its mathematical quantities are synthetic inputs, not money or measured performance. Preserve the distinction in captions and graphics. Reopen Phase 1 if the script later adds an economic or benchmark claim. See [research applicability](../02_Phase1_RESEARCH_BIBLE/Financial_Figures_Verified.md).


### Delivery plan and execution checklist
#### Deliverables and editorial specifications
Current deliverable is a source-backed text/scene/prompt package prepared for external AI generation. Proposed final video target: 600 seconds, 1920×1080, 16:9, 24 fps. Proposed review export: MP4/H.264 with stereo AAC at 48 kHz; preserve a high-quality master as chosen in the actual editing workflow. These are project choices; check the chosen generator/editor and platform before execution.

Supply a separate EN transcript, timed SRT captions after recording/edit, thumbnail, final metadata and source list. Optional short-form versions need their own truthful framing and reviewed captions.

#### Pre-generation text checks
Configuration locks, full narration, synthetic quantities, source limitations, continuity, beat/chapter plan and planned asset identities are specified. Phase 3 still must instantiate scenes, cite actual factual wording, validate timing and supply concrete prompts. Phase 4 must compile a current package; Phase 5 must audit the resulting text.

#### Final export checks — Pending actual media
- Measure narration fit for each scene; revise and log overruns.
- Inspect every generated visual for misleading evidence, identity/shape drift and incorrect text.
- Render exact overlays and verify fractions, order and source labels.
- Align captions to actual speech, review author names and mathematical phrasing, and check readability.
- Measure duration, frame rate, dimensions, audio loudness, clipping and final synchronization.
- Inspect thumbnail, cards, chapters and metadata against the actual edit.
- Confirm actual rights/provenance and platform disclosure decisions.

#### Release gate
No export, upload, license clearance or publication has occurred. CONFIG human review stays Pending. Release requires the actual reviewers, their evidence and all applicable checks, including media checks after the user generates it. Planning completion cannot certify unseen media.


## 11. Production Notes

### Voiceover — Engaging Storytelling
One calm EN narrator. Explain rather than announce a revelation. Emphasize “invented,” “within the studied systems,” and “before normalization.” Read the script exactly within its evidence scope; do not improvise product examples or claims.

#### Timing and delivery
75 cues occupy planned eight-second slots. See 11_Dialogue_Script.md for spoken text and PRODUCTION_MANIFEST.json (narration_plan) for its machine-readable equivalent. Plan around 150–175 words per minute, with shorter cues leaving reading space. Equations and names require actual rehearsal; word count is not a recorded-duration measurement. Aim to finish each cue within its slot and use remaining time for a visual hold.

If a cue overruns, first reduce wording while preserving claim scope and arithmetic. Log the change in both script records, retain eight-second scene duration, then repeat narration review. Do not speed the entire explanation merely to fit a dense passage.

#### Pronunciation and accessibility
Read NLP as individual letters; arXiv as “archive”; ln as “natural log”; q as “query” when introducing notation. Use “one half,” “one quarter,” “two thirds” and “four thirds.” Verify author-name pronunciation before recording; do not claim a pronunciation was approved.

A licensed voice or the user's own voice may be used. Named researchers are never impersonated. Provide an accurate transcript and synchronized captions after generation. The visual description must remain understandable without relying exclusively on color or movement.


### Music, sound and silence
#### Mix intent
Use clear speech as the foreground. A restrained non-vocal ambient bed may support section transitions. Prefer silence during the densest arithmetic. Minimal soft clicks can mark a reveal; use no dramatic alarms or “discovery” stingers implying empirical revelation.

#### Targets and monitoring
Editorial mix targets: stereo master around -16 LUFS integrated and true peak no higher than -1 dBTP, subject to actual listening and loudness measurement after generation/edit. These are chosen production targets, not a statement of platform requirements. Duck the optional music under speech and check voice intelligibility on headphones, small speakers and mono playback.

No continuous music is required. Leave short silent holds for reading. Do not hide rushed speech under a louder mix.

#### Rights and status
Music and SFX are planned. Record creator/provider, license, actual file, permitted usage and attribution in the asset register before use. No license or measured level exists yet. If generated music is used, assess the actual YouTube disclosure requirements with the finished media. Final mix, rights check and listening review remain Pending execution.


### Episode scene format
Phase 3 instantiates 75 actual scenes in three batches of 25. The entries in PRODUCTION_MANIFEST.json (narration_plan) are planned cue/scene slots, not completed scene records.

#### Required fields and source of values
| Field | Episode decision |
|---|---|
| Scene number / duration | Planned slot number; 8 seconds |
| Chapter | Matching STORY_BEAT_MAP.md section |
| Location | WORLD-01 editorial workspace; BOARD-01 for numbers |
| Time of Day / Period | Timeless explanatory set; source history and later research attributed separately |
| Characters | Abstract A/B/C markers when applicable; narrator off-screen |
| Visual Direction | One primary operation, defined continuity and reference asset IDs |
| Data Visualization Specs | Exact applicable stage from 06_Data_Visualization_Specs.md, or a justified absence of numerical graphics |
| Dialogue / Narration | Exact matching cue from 11_Dialogue_Script.md |
| Voiceover Duration | Planned fit within 8 seconds; actual measurement Pending generation |
| Claim IDs / Source Citation | Cue claim IDs and exact ledger source locator; editorial-only question explicitly identified |
| Legal Risk Flag | Attribution/interpretation scope when relevant; human release review remains Pending |
| SFX / Music Cue | Sparse and subordinate to speech, with silent arithmetic holds |
| Transition to Next Scene | Continuity-preserving cut or simple reveal |
| AI generation prompt | Scene-linked prompt, reference IDs, motion, exclusions, intended start/end state |
| Asset IDs | Planned register references, later mapped in manifest |

Editorial-only scenes must state that they add no factual assertion; do not invent a claim ID. Factual overlays must carry the same evidence references as narration.

#### Worked planned slot 33
Eight-second BOARD-01 shot; A/B/C remain ordered. Highlight B value (0,4) and weight 1/4, then reveal its contribution (0,1). Keep A's prior contribution visible and C unchanged. Narration uses cue 33. Claims C011/C012, S1 equation 1 plus local arithmetic. Use G03, G04 and V01 references. No camera move during the equation; transition to the sum. This is a filled planning example, not rendered scene evidence.

#### AI prompt recipe
“WORLD-01, BOARD-01, original abstract vector markers, fixed overhead framing, one controlled reveal, high contrast, stable empty overlay zones. Preserve A square / B circle / C triangle positions. No lettering, numerals, researcher likeness, logos, photographic evidence or interface screenshots. Start and finish in the states defined by this scene's editorial layer.”

At generation, adapt duration/input syntax to the selected provider's current capabilities. Record actual tool/version/settings; split clips if needed while preserving the parent scene's eight-second editorial timing.
